**Load-book entries 264 to 273. The measured figures and the matchers that produced them are in `workspace/volume-06/batch-0002/SUMMARY.md` sections 2 and 6. No day number, month-name, day-date or year appears in any of the ten chapters.**

### Chapter 0261 — "A Room Cannot Come Back"

**The Monday morning of week ninety-two, a room in Crown Terrace, and a service road in the wet. Load-book entry 264, whose own opening label says this entry is one item, seven smaller questions, and sixty-three days.** Eleven on the docket and eleven in date; a stair fire door not reported and not chased. Room a hundred and sixty-eight days old, card a hundred and seventy-two, heating on its sixty-third day, twelve lines eighty-eight, thirteen thirty-nine, fourteen four. **The item was heard: whether a practice can be required to show that any person in it may stop at any time, and what evidence would satisfy that.** A chair of forty-nine whose office signs about eleven of a kind a year asked seven questions, each smaller than the one before, and the fourth of them was the word budget out loud before the sentence finished. Aurelia Sopp, sixty, gave the finding she had given a fortnight earlier in a corridor — the only evidence is a record of who stopped, a record of who stopped is a column, about eleven thousand premises have no column — was met with the smaller question *who would keep the column*, and the item went ahead anyway. **Talia Venn gave about eleven minutes on a practice she has been inside of for a term and a half and did not read the sentence on the wall binder.** He described the room from a chair in nine sentences and was not standing in front of anything. **The one thing produced was nine words at about eleven twenty-five: a room cannot ask to be heard again and a person has to.** Nobody thanked her. The clerk of forty-four is still seconded to the secretariat and the chair was not told; he saw the shape of it at a lift and did not raise it.

### Chapter 0262 — "A Hearing Is A Book"

**The Wednesday morning of week ninety-two, the twelfth sitting of the Exchange in a room above the line in Saltmarket, and a cold room off a service road in the evening. Load-book entry 265, one unopened book, one sign that changed, and sixty-five days.** Stair fire door reported Monday, attended, reset for the eighth time in about a fortnight, no fault that will go in a box. Room a hundred and seventy days old, card a hundred and seventy-four, heating sixty-five, twelve lines ninety, thirteen forty-one, fourteen six. **A man of about forty-four asked that the Monday hearing be entered in the book and was refused, and the reason given is the first in nineteen years that is about the room: a hearing is a book, and a book of what a caller said cannot be written by the people who did not call, and not one person in that hearing had telephoned that desk or attended that room.** The book did not open and is at forty-six lines. **The telephone count moved from forty-four to forty-seven; she did the arithmetic in her head and refused to do it again; the sign changed from the book two lines ahead to the telephone one line ahead; she said the direction out loud once and did not look into it; and she asked that no person present convert the two figures into one another or into a number of sittings.** The count is now announced seventeen times, twelve of them on the twelve Wednesdays. **She did not ring the one caller who is not coming**, and gave the reason, and he did not come. The ninth chair is against the wall, not moved back, its mover not named, its holder a young woman of twenty-one in attendance since fifteen who was not asked anything. The young man of twenty-one keeps a count and was given the answer about furniture for the fifth time and was not to be confirmed and was not to be stopped.

### Chapter 0263 — "Four Times Over"

**The Thursday of week ninety-two, a delivery stoppage, and a sink. Load-book entry 266, one stoppage, four visits, and sixty-six days.** Eleven on the docket and ten in date; the door attended at about two, found exactly as described on eleven previous occasions; the basement water back to four inches with nothing done to it. Room a hundred and seventy-one days old, card a hundred and seventy-five, heating sixty-six, twelve lines ninety-one, thirteen forty-two, fourteen seven. **About nine drivers are stood down over a hand-held that records every handover; two of the nine say it is the most sensible thing their employer has ever asked of them, and one of those two is a woman of about forty-four with six years of driving for that company.** She came in at about half past six, asked whether she could sit, **was answered in four words, and sat for about forty minutes and was asked nothing.** She said one thing before leaving: that she came because it is the only room in this city where nobody has ever asked her to justify being in it. **At about half past seven a first-year of nineteen named the cost at a sink: four visits, four Thursdays, all of them in that room, and if they are friends then anybody who wants to know what she is doing on a Thursday afternoon has to find one person, and the one person is on about eleven hundred pieces of paper with her own name at the bottom by her own choice. She is going to stop being her friend for about a term.** Nobody improved on it, nobody thanked her, and about four people wanted to say it had not been her choice and none of them said it. The stoppage was settled in about four minutes nine days later by the company and its drivers, and nobody in this case was in that room.

### Chapter 0264 — "Cards Go In Pockets"

**The Friday of week ninety-two, a company training room off a corridor in Crown Terrace, and a cold room off a service road. Load-book entry 267, one question asked four times, one refusal of four words, and sixty-seven days.** Grade three, twenty to eight, on a card in date; eleven on the list and about four not on it; four jobs, the last a stair fire door reset for the ninth time in about a fortnight. Room a hundred and seventy-two days old, card a hundred and seventy-six, heating sixty-seven, twelve lines ninety-two, thirteen forty-three, fourteen eight. Nothing to hand out, four runs faster, all four ending on a spoken word that is on no card and never has been. **A woman of about thirty-four asked for the fourth time in a term to have the ending written down and was refused in four words — cards go in pockets — and the long form of the reason was given nine weeks earlier in about thirty words, was given a second time on being asked whether it had changed, and will not be given a third.** She said she would ask a fifth and is not asking because she thinks the refusal is wrong; she has finished a run by waiting about eleven seconds for four years and would like to stop. **On the last run nobody waited, in about four seconds, and whether that is the cards or the chair is not established.** A man of twenty-one near the door did not speak. The man of twenty-nine who works on lifts was not in that room and was not asked whether he was coming. Nobody thanked anybody.

### Chapter 0265 — "I Am Not In The Book"

**The Monday morning of week ninety-three, a room above the line in Saltmarket with no sitting in it. Load-book entry 268, one refusal of nine words, and seventy days.** Ten on the docket and ten in date; the basement reported for the tenth time inside about four weeks. Room a hundred and seventy-five days old, card a hundred and seventy-nine, heating seventy, twelve lines ninety-five, thirteen forty-six, fourteen eleven. **A hearing produced one thing and the item therefore came back on the Friday to the officer of sixty who found its fault and did nothing about it. She went to Saltmarket at about half past ten with a folder and asked, once, for the woman of sixty-one to come to a room in Crown Terrace and say what happens on the other side of her table.** The refusal was nine words, given in nine seconds and twice more in the same words: **she keeps the book and is not in the book.** The rest of the reason was given once and withdrawn — in about four years a description of nineteen years would make every one of those people a thing that was described, and not one of them can object, and she is not going to carry it into a building. **Nobody thanked her. Nobody asked a second question, nobody proposed a smaller thing, nobody offered a car, nobody offered to come to her, nothing was written down, and about four people noticed that nobody had said that somebody ought to go and get her.** She is not carried anywhere, is not put on a stage, is not described as a resource, and is not asked again. **He did not ask her to change her mind** and was asked why by a first-year of nineteen. Aurelia Sopp said once that she has been right every time she opens her mouth for nineteen years, that this morning the right thing was to be right, and that she held it back, and that she is not going to be thanked for it.

### Chapter 0266 — "Six Weeks Of Somebody"

**The Wednesday of week ninety-three, the object of the volume, and a cold room. Load-book entry 269, one figure, six weeks, and seventy-two days.** Eleven on the docket; the stair fire door back and reset; the basement down to two inches on its own. Room a hundred and seventy-seven days old, card a hundred and eighty-one, heating seventy-two, twelve lines ninety-seven, thirteen forty-eight, fourteen thirteen. **A man of fifty-one with a tool bag said nine hundred doors in four buildings carrying a number off a different line of the same list, the same figure for about four years, said in about four rooms, never thanked, and was not thanked.** **A woman of thirty-four, Petula Fenn, said the checking has to be done by a person because a number is on a wall and the list is on paper, that it is about four minutes a door and about nine where the lift does not work, that the list is reissued about four times a year and never corrected and arrives in a bag with nobody ringing anybody, and that there is no money for it and she is the person and it is her own time.** A first-year of nineteen did the arithmetic out loud three times: nine hundred at four minutes is sixty hours, four issues a year is two hundred and forty hours, **about six weeks of a working year, every year, for one person.** She has asked twice in writing and the second reply calls the list a guide. **Nobody asked to see the card. She did not produce it, on the ground that then it would be in the room.** The man of twenty-nine who works on lifts was not there, was not told and was not asked. Talia Venn said that she has never been asked to show anybody the card and that the only person who ever asked her about it asked in a corridor in about nine seconds, and that those two facts are one sentence.

### Chapter 0267 — "The Back Of His Own Hand"

**The Friday of week ninety-three, a telephone at ten past five in the morning, a company training room in the evening, and a service road in the dark. Load-book entry 270, one plate, one refusal, and seventy-four days.** Grade three, twenty to eight; eleven on the list, about four not on it; the stair fire door attended on the Thursday and reset for the tenth time in about a fortnight. Room a hundred and seventy-nine days old, card a hundred and eighty-three, heating seventy-four, twelve lines ninety-nine, thirteen fifty, fourteen fifteen. **A man of thirty-four who works nights found a plate in a service cupboard on a fourth landing in Nine Bridges, did not touch it, did not open the box behind it, did not photograph it, wrote nothing beside it and did not report it, and rang the number on a plate in the Lower Wards to say so.** It carries the block's own number, four things in a box on its face and no fifth, and it was not there in the spring and he was in that cupboard in the spring. **Nothing is wrong with it, and that is the whole of the finding.** He was asked what the fifth thing is and was not told, and the ground is that explaining it would make him the second person in this case who has been told. **He said in advance he would not say it twice and would not put it on paper, and that if it were written down he would know; he was told it would not be; he said it would be, and that he would rather be written down than asked twice.** Nobody has ever asked him anything. On the rota, **the woman of about thirty-four was on the list for the first time in four years, asked nothing, and finished a run in about four seconds, and nobody in that room said anything and nobody was thanked.**

### Chapter 0268 — "Four Hundred Hands"

**The Monday of week ninety-four, Crown Terrace, and a cold room. Load-book entry 271, one sheet, about two hundred and forty hands, and seventy-seven days.** Ten on the docket and ten in date; a call in Nine Bridges closed by somebody else before he arrived. Room a hundred and eighty-two days old, card a hundred and eighty-six, heating seventy-seven, twelve lines a hundred and two, thirteen fifty-three, fourteen eighteen. **A man of fifty-one, on a job and not looking for it, found a sheet about the size of a piece of kitchen paper taped to a service corridor wall with about two hundred and forty lines on it in about two hundred and forty different hands, all the same four words, of which about four hundred exist in that one building over four years.** He did not photograph it. **The four words are a cook's and are the first line of the hardboard in that room, and she was at a sink in that room while it was all said, and nobody told her, and a first-year of nineteen gave nine words — they are hers, four hundred lines is a bigger surface — and then gave the whole ground when asked for the rest of it, and about nine people said nothing at the same time.** **The oldest hand is in the second column about nine lines down; two people in that room had seen it and neither said so; the man who wrote it was given an answer by a stranger about four years ago, was sixty-one, and has gone quiet; a room of about forty people in Crown Terrace knows he still has nine words of his own; and nobody in this case can hand him four words, because he cannot be found.** He is not named, nothing was sent to him, and nothing will be.

### Chapter 0269 — "Forty-One Names"

**The Wednesday of week ninety-four, a counter in Bower Street and a back room, and a cold room in the evening. Load-book entry 272, one page, forty-one names, nine minutes, and seventy-nine days.** Eleven on the docket; the stair fire door attended and reset for the eleventh time in about a fortnight. Room a hundred and eighty-four days old, card a hundred and eighty-eight, heating seventy-nine, twelve lines a hundred and four, thirteen fifty-five, fourteen twenty. **A clerk of fifty-three with nineteen years behind that counter read a page of about forty-one names out loud once, in about nine minutes, to about nine people, because a page nobody has read is a page nobody can close.** She said before she began that it was not going to do anything; she worked that out halfway down the list and read the other half anyway; nobody spoke between the names; and she put the sheet on the table writing side up, which is the only time in about four years it has been on a table. About nine of the forty-one were telephoned before whatever happened, one who was not was safe, and thirty-one have not been heard from. **A district officer asked whether it could be recorded properly with a reference and was refused: a page of forty-one cannot go into a system because it would work, and if it did not work she would be doing it.** The pencil box on that counter was not emptied, took no second name, was not given a reference, was not moved onto a system and was adopted by no district. She was thanked by nobody. A first-year of nineteen said one sentence in the evening and asked that nobody make it into a thing.

### Chapter 0270 — "The Last Thing He Wanted"

**The small hours of the Thursday of week ninety-four, a cold room, a hardboard, and a service road in the wet. Load-book entry 273, one line in fourteen words, one cost, and eighty days.** Eleven on the docket and ten in date; one job not attended, not reassigned and not reported. Room a hundred and eighty-five days old, card a hundred and eighty-nine, heating on its eightieth day and unpaid, twelve lines a hundred and five, thirteen fifty-six, fourteen twenty-one, **and the fifteenth zero, which is a difference and not an inclusive count.** About five people at half past one in the morning. **A man of thirty-four who works nights named the cost before he made the mark: that he is the only person in this case who can now be found at a door, that this is the last thing he has wanted for about four years, and that he knows that is a stupid thing to want.** He gave the mechanism himself — the call at ten past five, the refusal to have it written down, and that he would rather be written down than asked twice — and he gave the reason the cost is real, which is a plate in a service cupboard that is still there, that in about four years somebody will go into it, and that the only person who knows it exists is findable at a door. **He wrote fourteen words, unsigned, with a space left where a name would go, and about four people looked at it. The wall is fifteen lines in nine hands and ten names. Nobody signed it, nobody was thanked, nobody argued, and nobody asked him whether he was sure.** A first-year of nineteen said again that a line is not a rule and was told by the man who had written it that the difference has been said on that board four times and by about nine people and that she is none of them; the count was agreed as four. **On his way out he said that he is not going back into that cupboard, that nobody is going, that this is not a plan but four words at ten to two in the morning, and that somebody ought to write down before then that not going is a decision and not a thing that lasts — and was told that nobody is going to write that down, and said he knows, and that this is why he said it to a room and not to a man.** The woman of about thirty came in at about two and was not asked anything and nobody apologised to her.

### Chapter 0251 — "Folded Once"

**The Monday morning of week eighty-nine, from about half past nine to about ten at night, in a room off a service road in the Lower Wards and on a service road in the dark. Load-book entry 254, and its own opening label says this entry is one folded page, one number, and forty-two days.** Ten on the docket and nine in date, the tenth a stair fire door in Nine Bridges that has come up seven times in about a month with a stamped number in the description box, reset four times and checked twice and found as described every time. **The eighth thing went into a ring binder at about half past two without anybody asking for it:** a woman of about thirty, who has been in that room on every working day since the Friday of week sixty-nine and has been asked nothing in nineteen weeks, took a page folded once out of a bag on her lap, carried it to a shelf, opened the binder, put it in at the back, shut it, put it back and sat down, and said nine words addressed to the room and not to a person. Nobody asked her a question. **A first-year of nineteen told a man of twenty-two, in nine words and from the end chair with her boots off, that reading it makes you the person who read it, and then gave the reason unprompted, and the reason is not manners: four of us were watching you, and if you do it then you are a person who reads other people's pages, and that is a thing you can do again.** He asked what he was to do with it and was told nothing. The binder holds eight items, cannot be emptied, has been read standing up by one man of about thirty-one in about nine minutes, and no page has ever been taken out. **A cook of twenty-seven, at a sink, said she has asked about the heating three times, will not ask a fourth, that this is not a refusal but a Tuesday she is not going to spend again, and that this was the fourth time she had said a thing of that shape against a promise not to say a fifth, and she would rather she was the one wrong.** Nobody wrote it down. A man of fifty-eight with a bad hip came for an hour and a half, read the board and the unremovable grey ghost above it, declined tea and offered no comment. A man of thirty-four who works nights declined to produce his sheet of about nine addresses and said once that he was not going to talk about the door list again, and that the last time he had, he had talked for about four minutes and come away with the sense that he had done something, and that he had not.

### Chapter 0252 — "The Box In Pencil"

**The Wednesday morning of week eighty-nine, a ground-floor district practice standards office in District 3, and a service road in the wet. Load-book entry 255, and its own opening label says this entry is four fields, one date, and a binder on a wall.** Eleven on the docket and eleven in date, the first time in about a term that the two have been equal, and a basement's standing water reported for the fifth time at about twenty-two inches, into whose description box he wrote that the fault is not the pump and declined to state where the water is thought to come from. **A practice standards officer of District 3 of sixty, whose job it is to ask questions, asked a woman of twenty-four whether a person can take a share back without a form, and was told yes, in this city, in about four buildings, one of them by hand, by a clerk of fifty-three with nineteen years behind her, in a box ruled in pencil holding one name, with no reference number, not on a system, not a form, and which may not take a second name and which nobody in this city has ever asked her for.** And was told the four figures: a page of about forty-one people that cannot be closed from the outside, about nine of them telephoned before anything happened and one who was not and was safe; about four hundred and one replies in a company's general enquiries box of which about nine of the first forty-one got the company answer; about nine hundred doors in four buildings carrying a number off a different line of the same list; and a wall plate that says two-fourteen over a room that is two-oh-seven, with a fault ticket closed with the correct number in the building's own typeface by a man doing the job properly, **which changed nothing and is on the page as a correct action that changed nothing.** And was told that the standard on a practice card is real, numbered, dated eleven years, **not withdrawn**, that nothing in that binder has been withdrawn in nineteen years because withdrawing one is a thing a person writes on it, that she would be worried if it were not right, and that there is no conspirator in it and the question of who may invoke it is a question about about four hundred people and not about one. A standards officer named in this case since the eighth week of a term two years ago read a letter for a man in a car park and telephoned the woman who was to read it on the Friday in advance, and the man has not forgotten it and did not use it. **The man of twenty-two was in the corridor for the last four minutes and did not enter the room, said he had never seen one of those standards and would not know if one was put in front of him, was told that this was the most useful thing anybody in that building had said that morning and that he did not know he was saying it, described the hardboard, was told there was no use for it, and said that somebody had already photographed a thing in a room for a prospectus read by about four hundred people and he was not going to do it again.** The smaller question, asked in about nine seconds: are you going to say this again. Yes. Why. Because I want it, and wanting it is not a reason, and I would rather be told than have it noticed later — the first such statement in nineteen years in that room, against about eleven refusals of which about nine gave a reason and two of those were better.

### Chapter 0253 — "Three Buildings"

**The Thursday morning of week eighty-nine, in three buildings and then in the room off the service road. Load-book entry 256, and its own opening label says this entry is one sentence in three buildings, one card nobody read out, and a refusal.** A man of thirty-four who buys training gave his name at the door, which nobody in that room had ever done before and which about four people noticed, and said beforehand that nobody had asked him to repeat anything and that he had decided in about nine days that not repeating it was worse, and that the decision was about him and not about the room. He then said the first line on that hardboard, a cook's, without altering it, in three buildings, to about thirty people. **Thanked in the first, by a woman who meant it, who wrote it on a whiteboard uncredited and said out loud that she had done that. Ignored in the second, where about eleven people were all waiting for somebody else to move, and he named that as the difficult one. Asked to leave in the third, by a man who used the word *scope* twice, and he named the first as the occasion he was worst at.** **A first-year of nineteen said a whiteboard is worse than a photograph and smaller, because a photograph makes a picture and a whiteboard makes a thing that is in the building, and in about four years somebody will write a sentence under it and the two will be read together and neither will be about a cook.** He answered that a woman on about eleven hundred leaflets is a different problem from a photograph of a counter and that he had not known about the leaflets until that day, and she said that it was not the same and it was worse and it was smaller. He then said that he had carried it out of the room, that about nine people in three buildings now know a man of twenty-two holds a view about names, that none of them know which of them said it, and that the whole of the sentence is a cook's who would not want it out of that room and cannot stop it and has never once been asked — and he said he had done that to a woman he had never met because he did not want to be the person saying it, asked to be told he was wrong, said he did not think he would be, and was not told. **A company card is described and then stopped:** a starting thing, steps, a place where you let go, a printed box about what the practice must not touch, and nothing about how a person decides they are finished — and a man of twenty-nine who works on lifts instructed him to stop and gave his standing reason for the second time in this city, that if he goes and looks then in about four years there is a list of buildings and a list of buildings is a list of people, and added that the eleven are a different sentence, that he has no reason for the eleven, and that he will not make one up in a room on a Thursday. **A woman of twenty-four put the whole argument in about thirty words: a sentence in a room is nine people, a sentence outside a room is nobody's, those are the two things there are, and you cannot build a third thing out of the two.** Nobody thanked anybody.

### Chapter 0254 — "The Word He Did Not Say"

**The Friday of week eighty-nine, a company training room off a corridor in Crown Terrace with about nine other rooms on it, the corridor, and a service road with the shutter behind him. Load-book entry 257, and its own opening label says this entry is one release, one word, and forty-six days.** Grade three, twenty to eight, on a card in date, with the term's exception recorded and renewed in a system in a box that says *No reason given by holder*. Eleven on the Friday list for the week and about four of the eleven not on it, and the city's standing weekly figure is unaltered and is not a rate and is on the back of a woman's own card. Nine folding chairs, nine people, a laminated sheet on a post by the door carrying nine words with no name, no date, no company and no version, and a man of fifty-one with a bad wrist who came on a Friday and is not on the list. **The practice is a load-hold with a boundary-release at the end, and the beat is printed on all nine cards in a box in the same typeface as the box about what the practice must not touch, and the wording is correct. The instructor ran it through four times in about twenty minutes, ending each with one spoken word that is not *finished*, not *release*, and is not any word on the card.** Asked in a corridor what the word was for, she said to confirm the release, and that the card is what somebody wrote down and the room is what people actually do, and that in about four years a person will be sitting in a chair waiting and she is the person they wait for and she would like to be able to say why that is a good idea. Asked whether he thought it was a good idea: very good at not going wrong, which is not the same, and she is thirty-six and has done it for nine years. **On the last run a woman of about twenty-two completed the hold, reached the release, stopped, consulted a card that carries the beat, and waited; two counts of about eleven seconds were taken, one by him and one at the back; the instructor said the word and the practitioner finished and the room restarted. He was in the fourth row, had the word available, could have produced it at any point in those eleven seconds, and did not. Nobody in the room or the corridor remarked on it and nobody thanked him for it.** A man of fifty-one with a bad wrist said, to nobody, that she will go home and practise and wait about four times and the fourth will be in a stairwell on her own with nobody there and will then work without her noticing, and that this is the good outcome and the bad outcome and is the same thing, and that he has written a version of it down for a company and been told it is not a field, and that the field is blank and the person who licensed it is not named, and that he has been trying for about two years to find out who they were and has stopped asking and has not stopped noticing. **At about half past four on a service road he said the reason out loud, standing up, to nobody, and said it twice to check it, and the reason is that he is the man in the lanyard, and that in about nine minutes in a room in Crown Terrace he was the only other person in that building who could have made a woman of twenty-two finish and did not, because he does not know what happens to a person who finishes it alone and does not know she has done something she should not have been able to do.**

### Chapter 0255 — "Four Parts And Not Five"

**The Monday morning of week ninety, the same district office, and a service road in the wet. Load-book entry 258, and its own opening label says this entry is one card, one typeface, and forty-nine days.** Ten on the docket and nine in date, and a basement's standing water at about sixteen inches, down from about twenty-two, reported for the seventh time in about four weeks, with no stopcock, valve or inlet fitted by anybody. **A clerk of forty-four, who is the person who answers the officer's questions and not her instrument, was named at the counter in the ordinary way and about four people heard it.** She brought a plastic wallet with about nine cards and a folded sheet listing printings, and said the cards are the company's and not hers, that they come in a pack of about four hundred and go out to about eleven sites, and that the one on the top is not the company's. **The card names all five parts of a practice — a starting hold, ordered steps, a point of release, a printed box stating what the practice must not touch, and a statement that an aftertaste is normal — and contains nothing whatever about how a practitioner knows they are finished.** She said she has carried it since about a term and a half and brought it because a person of sixty asked her a question in that building. He warned her not to say it too quickly, so that nobody starts looking for who put it there, and she said she has not said it quickly. **He then turned five copies of the same card over: the hold, the steps and the release are one typeface in all five; the boundary box is the same typeface in four and a different one at half a point larger in the fifth, in circulation about eleven years; the revision column dates that change eleven years and the four earlier printings are older, and no line anywhere on the sheet carries a note of withdrawal, amendment, reason or reviewer.** The officer said out loud that she wanted it noted that she did not ask whether a company was hiding something, because in about four weeks somebody will say she did. **A man of twenty-nine who works on lifts, told the finding in four sentences, asked nothing, and restated his standing refusal before the question was asked. A first-year of nineteen stated the room's position: we are not going to write that down anywhere, and if somebody writes it down it is a record of a card, and a record of a card says who read the card. A man of thirty-four who buys training answered that he would rather be findable than be the ninth in a row who is not.** And the clerk of forty-four then said, before leaving and unprompted, that she worked in Crown Terrace for about two years a long time ago in an office with nothing to do with this one, that she would remember the name of a room if it were said to her and nothing else, that there were about nine people in it and she typed for all of them, and that she was not important and neither was the typing — and gave the reason for saying it in the room rather than a corridor: that in about four years somebody will find out that a clerk in this district used to type in a building about two hundred metres from where she works, and she would rather it came from her on a Monday. No name was asked for and none was given, and no room, no floor and no date appears in the chapter.

### Chapter 0256 — "Nine Hundred Cards"

**The Wednesday morning of week ninety, a district office, about eleven sites over four days, and a cold room off a service road at about half past six. Load-book entry 259, and its own opening label says this entry is one rule, nine seconds, and about nine hundred.** Ten on the docket and ten in date, and a stair fire door in Nine Bridges for the eighth report in about a month, attended at about three, reset for the sixth time in about a fortnight, expected back. **It took about nine seconds and there is no part of the account anybody would argue with. PS-3-11(c), dated eleven years, not withdrawn, a practice card is not to be issued unless it carries a beat that performs the boundary — and the clerk who said it said before saying it that she did not want to be the one who said it out loud, that this was exactly why she had to, and that in about four years it would be in a newspaper with a photograph of the person who did it on it, and that she is the person who did it. Anybody in that office can invoke it on a Monday without asking anybody. It is not hers. Nobody thanked her. She has never been thanked.** About four days, about nine hundred cards, about eleven sites, and the figure is a figure of objects and not of people and she said so aloud in about nine buildings before she said it anywhere else. **The checking was done by a man of thirty-four who buys training, on his own time, on four days his employer does not know he took and has not been told, driving between about eleven sites against a printed column, thanked at nine and asked at two by a man with a clipboard whether he was from a company, and at the eleventh he said that it is the company that supplies them.** The four days found the two-typeface card in nine of about eleven sites; four of the nine locally reprinted by a printer told he was correcting a misprint, in one of which a man of fifty-eight with a bad hip printed two hundred himself above a fish market, for nothing, out of a habit he cannot stop; about nine people on no rota across four of the sites, two of whom have never been asked whether they want to be there, one being a man of fifty-one with a bad wrist who attends on a Friday and is not on the list; **and, in about nine minutes of a Friday afternoon, a card that had already been withdrawn still on a lanyard round a woman's neck while a person who had not read the list was about to tell her that her release was held and current.** Nobody was hurt, the release was held and current, the woman said the outcome was correct and the hour was wrong, and she was thanked by nobody. **A man of twenty-nine who works on lifts said, unprompted, in about nine words, that the four days were the right four days, that not one of those cards remains in about eleven buildings, and that what is actually happening in about nine of those buildings has not changed and cannot be changed by taking a piece of paper out of a drawer.** He was answered yes and said nothing else. **The clerk of forty-four named the cost of the day nobody is paying for, in a corridor, on the second morning, to one person, in about nine seconds and once: the pulling is one day a week, it is not in her job, nobody is paying for the day, nobody has asked her to stop, and if she stops then in about four years the standard will still be on that wall and nobody will be pulling cards.**

### Chapter 0257 — "Two Sets Of Nine Hundred"

**The Friday of week ninety, the second Friday rota, a company training room in Crown Terrace with eleven people in nine chairs, and a cold room off a service road. Load-book entry 260, and its own opening label says this entry is two figures of nine hundred, one question nobody had asked in nine years, and fifty-three days.** Grade three from twenty to eight on a card in date, eleven on the Friday list and about four of the eleven not on it, the city's standing weekly figure unaltered, and a stair fire door in Nine Bridges for the ninth report in about a month, attended at about half past three, reset for the seventh time in about a fortnight, and found as described. **The cards were gone and the room had nothing to hand out.** The instructor handed in the last copy on the Thursday, unobjected to, and said twice that she wanted it on the record that she did not object, and that a card is a note and you take the note, and that she has been able to name the five parts aloud for nine years and has never once had to. **The four runs went faster, and the reason is hers and is correct: nine people were not looking at a piece of paper. The runs ended on the same spoken word they have ended on for nine years, and that word is the beat which ends a practice and hands the choice back to the practitioner, and it is on no card, has never been on a card, and is not in the box on the post by the door.** A woman of about thirty-four in the second row asked whether the ending could be written down for her to check herself against, and was refused, on one reason used three times rather than three reasons: a sheet becomes a card, a card becomes something in a pocket, and a pocket becomes a session somebody runs for a person who has not got the pocket. **A man of twenty-nine who works on lifts then said the day's actual finding, to the floor: there were nine of us last Friday and there are eleven this week, nobody was told, nobody came because of a card, he does not know one of the eleven faces from any other Friday, he is not going to ask, and if nine hundred people stop being handed a piece of paper and eleven of them turn up instead then that is not an improvement and it is not nothing, and we will not find out which for about four years.** Nobody thanked him. In the cold room a man of fifty-one with a tool bag put the company's own list of the eleven on the counter and established that four of those eleven sites are buildings he has put a number on, and that **the same figure — about nine hundred — has now come out twice in this case for two different sets of objects and nobody in this city can tell them apart in under about four minutes, and that he has wanted that written down for about four years and has never once wanted it written more than he wanted it not to be true.** Nobody wrote it down. A man of twenty-nine who works on lifts was asked, before he had sat down, whether he would go to two of the eleven sites and stand in them for one session each, and said: **No. I said why once and that is all.** Nine words, and not the reason. He is not to be asked again and is not to be thanked. **The rate reached eleven for about four minutes at about ten past four, the second time in about a term and a half, the first having been while about eleven buildings were cleared in standing water. He told nobody, and at about ten on a service road he said the number out loud once to an empty road, and he records that he does not know why a fortnight of water and a room that got two people through the door are the same size, and that the not knowing is the part of it he has.**

### Chapter 0258 — "Nine Seconds, And Then A Letter"

**The Monday morning of week ninety-one, a district office, a cold room off a service road, and a service road in the wet. Load-book entry 261, and its own opening label says this entry is one refusal, four minutes at a counter, and fifty-six days.** Ten on the docket and nine in date, and a basement's standing water reported for the eighth time at about nine inches, down from about sixteen, with nothing done to it by anybody, and he recorded the fall and attributed it to nothing. **A company telephoned the number on the plate at about half past nine**, asked for about forty minutes and said it would bring two people, and gave its name and its company before its reason, which is a thing a person does when the reason is going to sound bad. **A woman of about forty-three with a company lanyard said that her company supplies about eleven of the sites in question, that on Wednesday somebody in a district office pulled about nine hundred of its cards out of use in about four days on the authority of a piece of paper on a wall, that this was the correct thing to do and she is not here about that, and that her employer asked her on Friday whether there is anything in that room that is a problem and she has to write an answer and cannot write an answer about a thing she has not been in — and then said that the request is entirely reasonable, that she would make it, and that this is why she is nervous.** She came at about half past two with one other person, stood in the gap between the counter and the hardboard, looked at the four words in chalk and the unremovable grey ghost for about nine seconds and did not ask what either said, and said unprompted that she has a photograph of that counter in a bag in a car and has looked at it more than her own and that this is a filthy thing to say to a room — and a cook of twenty-seven said it was a filthy thing to say and the first true thing anybody had said at that door in about four months. **A first-year of nineteen refused in about nine seconds: a room which lets a company in is a room that gets quoted, a room that gets quoted is a room that gets asked about, and in about four years somebody will stand in that doorway and say the room agreed to something, and there is no way in this city to make them take it back out of the sentence — and that a company is a company and is not a problem and did nothing wrong, which is exactly why it cannot be in here, and that both readings of the request come to the same answer.** The visitor said it was the cleanest refusal she had been given and that she did not think it was right, and was told to say the rest. **Then nobody could work out who was going to answer the letter.** There is no name over the door, no committee, no minute, no officer and no post, and a written refusal requires a person at the bottom of it, and there were about eleven people in the room and one of them had a name anybody outside that building could use. The cook declined in one sentence. The man of twenty-nine declined in four words and gave no reason and was not asked. The first-year gave the standing position — whoever writes it owns it and is findable and the room will neither stop them nor help them — and then declined to write it, because she is the one person in that room who knows exactly what her own handwriting on about eleven hundred pieces of paper does to a person. **So he wrote it: about four sentences on the company's own notepaper, standing up at the counter, in about four minutes, signed, and read back to the room before it was sealed, which nobody had asked him to do, because a thing you are going to be asked about for four years ought to have been said in front of the people it is about.** The letter says no, thanks you for asking, we are not able to say why, and that if the company wants to be useful in any of this it can go and find the about nine people in those sites who are not on anybody's rota and put somebody in with them. A cook of twenty-seven said that was a good letter and the worst thing that has happened in that room in about four months, and that both of those are about the signature. **The visitor was given four figures out loud once in about nine minutes by a woman of twenty-four standing with her hands behind her back, wrote them on the back of her own company's card, and left no copy because the room does not keep things. She said it does not help her, that what she has to write is that she has seen it, and named her own cost: a file note recording a declined approach, which inside her own employer is a thing she can be found about, and a stated preference for writing it herself rather than have it written about her by the person above her. Nobody thanked her. The company sends nothing further this term.**

### Chapter 0259 — "A Question With One Answer"

**The Wednesday morning of week ninety-one, a district office in District 3, and a cold room off a service road at about half past seven with about nine people and then about eleven. Load-book entry 262, and its own opening label says this entry is one item, two faults in it, and fifty-eight days.** Ten on the docket and ten in date, a stair fire door for the tenth time in about a month attended at about eleven and reset, and a basement not reported since the Monday and not chased, and the entry records that it has not been chased. **The item is four paragraphs, handed to the officer on the Friday, and the question beneath them is whether a practice can be required to show that any person in it may stop at any time, and what evidence would satisfy that.** The hearing is set for the Monday of week ninety-two, one morning, in a room in Crown Terrace with about nine chairs. **She read the item twice, read the standard off the wall binder, and then said it out loud once, standing up, in about nine seconds and not in a corridor, and it was two faults.** One: the woman who pulled about nine hundred cards out of use on Wednesday is now seconded to the secretariat of the committee that is going to hear this, because a committee with an item needs a person to prepare it, and she is the only person in either building who knows what it cost to say it out loud, and the chair has not been told. Two: a practice cannot show that a person may stop at any time, because the only way to show it is to keep a record of who stopped, and a record of who stopped is a column, and a column is the one thing a room like that does not have, and a hearing where the answer is always no is not a hearing, it is a way of producing a finding. She was asked what she wanted to do about it and said nothing, and gave the reason: she is not the person who can do anything, has done nothing, and is speaking so that in about four years nobody can say an officer of that district sat on it. **And then she said the thing she has said once in nineteen years: that she has been right every time she has opened her mouth in that building, that this is not a qualification, that this is the difficulty, and that if a person is ever in a room with her and she is right then that is information about her and none of it is about the matter in front of them.** Nobody wrote it down and the clerk of forty-four, who is in the room, did not look up. **In the evening a woman of twenty-four, who had been asked on the Tuesday in a corridor and had asked for a day, refused before anybody asked her anything to be the person who says it out loud, named the sentence she will not say, and said she knows it is not hers and that not knowing whose it is is a different problem.** Reason given the room, in about nine seconds: if I am the person who says it then the only thing anybody can argue with becomes me; the rule is printed, numbered, dated, hangs on a wall and can be invoked by any competent person on a Monday without asking anybody, and none of that is me; the minute I say it in a room where it matters it becomes a thing a nurse of twenty-four said, and in about four years the sentence anybody reads will be mine and will be true and will also be the only sentence, and then the rule will have got a face on it — and a number on a wall cannot be cross-examined and a person can. **Then she gave the reason under the reason, unprompted, and said so before she said it: she is tired; she has been asked to be findable three times in a term and a half and has said no three times and this is the fourth; tiredness is not a reason and she is going to use it anyway; she would like to be somewhere that is not the answer to the question; and she has been pretending for about a day that a better reason was going to arrive.** **He argued for about four minutes and was wrong:** a clerk reading a number into a record is the only account of the standard that will reach about four hundred competent people on the Monday of week ninety-two, she is the only person he has met who has been inside one of these and come out of it, and if she does not describe what a card does to a person then the description will be a sheet with two typefaces on it and the sheet will be found sufficient. She answered that the card will be read by whoever is clerk of that hearing and read correctly, because it is written down, which is the whole reason it is written down, and that this is the only answer that does not put a person in the middle of a thing no person is required to stand in the middle of. **A first-year of nineteen said it from the end chair with her boots off in nine words: he is wrong, you are right, leave it there — and that he has been wrong about this for a term, said in the same breath as the other six words and not as a separate finding. He accepted it in about four seconds, did not say he would not improve on it, did not repeat it, and did not argue. Nobody thanked him for not arguing.** And she said the last of it to the room and not to him: the evidence she would have given was worth a lot, it is not going to be given, the item will go ahead on a worse record, that is the correct outcome, she is the one who has cost this room the better record, and nobody is to tell her it was not her choice.

### Chapter 0260 — "A Line Is Not A Rule"

**The Thursday evening of week ninety-one, a cold room off a service road from about half past seven until the shutter came down at about ten, and a service road in the wet. Load-book entry 263, and its own opening label says this entry is one line in fourteen words, one cost, and fifty-nine days.** Eleven on the docket and ten in date, a stair fire door for the eleventh time in about a month, attended and reset, and a basement reported for the ninth time at about four inches and found to have fallen again with nothing done to it, and he recorded the fall and attributed it to nothing. **The board over the door carried four words in chalk in the hand of a woman of about sixty-eight and, above them, a grey ghost of four in a sign-writer's hand that has been there since a Friday in week eighty-seven and cannot be got off it, and nobody in that room has ever tried twice. Underneath, on two nails, a hardboard the size of a tea tray carried thirteen lines in seven hands and eight names, the first a cook's and still the first, and the thirteenth a first-year of nineteen's and about five weeks old and an opt-out — and the hardboard is not the board over the door and is not the wall list in its plastic sleeve in a repair shop four districts away, and there are three objects and none has ever been merged with either of the others.** A woman of twenty-four stood up at about nine without asking and named the cost out loud before the mark: that her name is then on a surface in a building with a plate on the outside of it carrying a telephone number and no name, that such a plate is how about eleven things have got to that door in about a term, that in about four years somebody may stand in a room and photograph that hardboard and her handwriting will be on it and she will not be able to take it off, and that she has said no to being findable three times in a term and a half and this is not one of the three. She was asked for the other part and said she had written it the previous night and told nobody, because the moment she tells somebody it is a thing somebody else knows, and she has been the thing somebody else knows for a term and a half and is tired of it. **She read it out: that she is not going to be the person who is asked about this room.** **A first-year of nineteen said that is a line, and was told she knows it is a line, and then said the other thing out loud, because in about four years somebody who has not been in that room will read it and take it for a rule, and the only reason they will is that nobody said the difference — and then gave the whole difference without being asked: a rule has a number and a date and hangs on a wall in a building with a lot of paper in it and any competent person in this city can pick it up on a Monday and use it without asking anybody, and that one has been in force for eleven years and nobody has ever withdrawn it; that has two hands and one of them is hers. A line somebody wrote on a piece of hardboard costs its writer the thing she just said, does not bind one person including her, can be taken off with a thumb in front of nine people, and is evidence of nothing in any proceeding, there being none.** The woman of twenty-four said she did not know that on Wednesday, that she had believed for a term and a half that the two were the same thing, and asked that it be entered; she was told nobody is entering anything; she said then she wanted it said, and it had been said, and that would do. A cook of twenty-seven priced it from a sink at a kettle — fourteen words is a lot to pay for a piece of hardboard, and the trade is that it is on a wall instead of in a head, and she has been making it for about fourteen weeks with a kettle. **Nobody improved on the cost, nobody said it was worth it and nobody said it was not, and she asked for that and was not asked twice. She wrote it in about nine seconds with a pen off the counter, unsigned, with a space left where a name would have gone, and about four people looked at it. A man of fifty-one with a tool bag read it from the other side of the room and said nothing at all.** Nobody signed it and nobody was asked to, **and he did not come to the board at all, which is the first time in a term and a half that a line has gone on that hardboard and he has not been within four feet of it; he was at the counter with a docket the whole time, about four people noticed, and he did not explain it and was not asked.** A woman of about thirty was in the chair nearest the door from about two until the shutter came down, with a cup of tea put in front of her and no words, and nobody asked her anything and nobody apologised to her, because an apology would have been a question and it would still be one. The city's standing weekly figure stands unaltered and is not a rate and is on the back of a woman's own card. The heating is on its fifty-ninth day, unpaid, unresolvable by any document, and **no fund, collection, subscription, grant, list, minute, notice or appeal exists and none was proposed by anybody on that evening.**

---

# VOLUME 05 — MOVEMENT V (Chapters 241–250), "Three Districts" — COMPLETE, AND THE VOLUME IS COMPLETE ON THE PAGE

**`outline/volume-05.md` governs and it was not re-argued. The load-book run is 244 to 253, continuous, one entry per chapter, no duplicate and no gap, and IT ENDS HERE AT 253. The ten days are fixed in `workspace/volume-05/batch-0005/PROMPT.md`: two in week eighty-three, four in week eighty-four, one in week eighty-five, one in week eighty-six, one in week eighty-seven and one in week eighty-eight, with the Exchange on the Wednesdays of weeks eighty-four and eighty-eight and the Friday rota on the Fridays of weeks eighty-three, eighty-four and eighty-seven. No day number, month-name, month-date, day-date or year appears in any of the ten files.**

**THE ARITHMETIC SPINE OF THE MOVEMENT: the heating goes off on the Monday of week eighty-three and does not come back in this volume; a flood comes into the low end of three districts on the Thursday of week eighty-four; four people each run one piece of it and refuse once each, in public, on purpose; a first-year of nineteen removes the man of twenty-two from the only thing he owns in nine words on the Monday of week eighty-five; nine keys are needed and two exist, one unreachable and one removable in about nine seconds, and the answer was given aloud and not written down; the wall gains a thirteenth line on the Thursday of week eighty-six and loses two boards' worth of words; the board over the door is rubbed off with a thumb and written again in chalk by a stranger on the Friday of week eighty-seven; and the Exchange's book goes from forty-five lines to forty-six on the Wednesday of week eighty-eight and a woman of sixty-one will not open it on a Friday. The batch's own account, with the measured figures and the four passes, is `workspace/volume-05/batch-0005/SUMMARY.md`.**

### Chapter 0241 — "The Heat Is Off"

**The Monday morning of week eighty-three, from about twenty to eight in the morning to about ten at night, at a depot, in a room off a service road in the Lower Wards, and on a service road in the dark. Load-book entry 244, and its own opening label says this entry is a fortnight, a milk order, and a kettle.** Nine on the docket and nine in date, a bin-store timer that is not the fault, and a stair fire door in Nine Bridges whose closer he adjusted for the fourth time in about a fortnight and which came up carrying a stamped number in the box again and which he did not go and look at, having looked three times. **The heating in that room was off at seven in the morning and nobody had paid for it and nobody was going to.** The board over the door carried four words in a sign-writer's hand and, above them, eight in a cook's — *it is warmer in here than out there* — and the eight were untrue from the first hour of the day, which is what a first-year of nineteen had said out loud on the Friday. **The cook of twenty-seven set her kettle going at its fixed point in the day, said the distinction once and would not say it again that week, and gave two numbers off a scrap she keeps for the milk order: eleven cups on the Friday, seven on the Monday, and said the movement in the number is a movement in the number of people who are cold and not in the number of people.** Three of the four people who came for the temperature did not attend on the Monday and none has attended since, and no person in that room stated that aloud and there is no instrument there by which it could be established. **A man of thirty-four who works nights, on his ninth attendance, asked for about four seconds: that somebody telephone him on the morning the heat goes off, not to ask him anything, so that he need not be the person who walks in and finds out. A cook of twenty-seven, at a sink, offered a standing Tuesday at about ten, refused thanks in advance, and said she had known for about three weeks that he could not stop her. He said he did not say he could stop her, and she said she had known that about him for about three weeks.** The man of twenty-two came at half past half past nine with no docket for the third Friday running, said he would not rub off the upper line on the board and would not stand near it, asked for that to be recorded and said there is nowhere to record it, and moved about four feet when a first-year of nineteen told him he was the only person in that room anybody would look at twice. A man of about sixty stood in the doorway at about ten past eight, said he had come for the temperature, observed there was none, came in and sat for about an hour, read both lines on the board, and offered no comment on either. Nothing was decided, minuted, posted, published or registered.

### Chapter 0242 — "A Sump Pump That Was Not The Fault"

**The Friday evening of week eighty-three, four days into an unheated fortnight, with about four days of rain on it. Load-book entry 245, and its own opening label says this entry is one pump, one list nobody asked for, and a tally of eight.** Ten on the docket and nine in date, and a sump pump in a basement in a district whose name is not in this book which ran nine hours on a night with no rain heavy enough to call for it, which has a float switch set by somebody who is not named here and was never asked, and which two people reported inside a week, both right and neither the fault. **He attended in the rain at about half past four in nine inches of standing water and wrote in the description box that the fault is not the pump and that the water is about nine inches deep against a wall with no opening on this side, and did not write where he thought it came from, and a district man of thirty-eight said it was the first entry in about five months that had stated what the fault was not and had declined to state what it was, and then said, "That is the first one that has told me it doesn't know."** A hardboard on two nails had a run of water across its lower third that a cook of twenty-seven declined to wipe, on the principle that a wall that has been rained on should look as though it has been rained on. **A man of fifty-one with a tool bag, in out of the wet, said he has put about nine hundred door numbers on buildings in this city in about nine years, that a number on a door finds a building and not a person, that in about four years somebody will put a number on that door that finds a person, and that he has wanted it written down for about four years and has never once wanted it written more than he wanted it not to be true.** A cook of twenty-seven said one word about it and would not say which word. **A man of thirty-eight in a district jacket came in hoodless and said the useful part unasked: that three districts take water off that row, two off a river about nine miles off and one off a hill; that he will not give a date, having been wrong about that twice; that the only list worth anything in a fortnight's time is a list of who is in which building; that he could produce one in about a day; that he is not asking; and that not knowing who is in a room is the only thing he has got.** Asked why not ask, he said because about nine people in there could be found in a day and he does not want to know and there is a difference. **A first-year of nineteen said the rest of it standing in about nine seconds: that such a list could be written in a day and would run to about four hundred names of which about half could not give a district; that the first line on that wall forbids it; and that no version of the man in the doorway could be made to forget four hundred names. He agreed, put his hood up, and left. Nobody thanked him.** A man came in at half past nine with his coat streaming, would not say what he had come out of, was given tea and a chair, said nothing that is recorded, and left into the rain at about ten.

### Chapter 0243 — "One Name For A Whole City"

**The Monday morning of week eighty-four, a week into the unheated fortnight, in that room, and on a service road in the wet. Load-book entry 246, and its own opening label says this entry is one request, nine words, and a telephone that somebody answered in another building.** Eleven on the docket and eleven in date; the basement pump reported a second time inside four days; standing water in that basement nineteen inches at the second attendance and the pump still not the fault. **A person of thirty-four who buys training for a company, in that room twice before and asking for nothing in either, asked for a name: a request that came from inside her own organisation on the Friday for a flood affecting three districts in about four days, of which she does not know the date and has declined to press for. She reported that she had asked in three buildings and that the answer in each was that no such person exists, and that the answer is correct in the way a locked door is correct.** He said the fifth set of nine words in this case, after the disclaimer and not before the answer — *one name for a whole city is a post* — and a cook of twenty-seven counted it aloud as five words and identified it as the ninth of a sentence she has heard him begin, and was not the person he was talking to. **Then about four minutes uninterrupted, with a docket in his hand: a key is not a post because the giver can take it back and the holder can be relieved; his own key cannot be taken back because a company is not a person and he has said that twice here and it has not been fixed; a name is a key left out on a counter; about four hundred people in this city have said *I was never in that room* this year and about four hundred could not take it back afterwards, and he is one of them and the card in the rail is the only thing he has made in this case; a person who answers a line cannot be relieved of answering it, and he would have to say *this is not my district* about nine hundred times before it stopped being true. He then invited four people to find a hole and said he would rather it happened in that room than on a service road. Nobody answered and he was not thanked.** What he offered instead, before he was asked twice, was the number on the plate: not his, not a name, whoever is in the room answers it, the person answering will not know the caller and will not ask for one, and a caller who wants a person cannot have one and must be a room. **A cook of twenty-seven named the consequence at a cloth and a sink: on a Tuesday at two in the morning the person who picks that up is whoever is standing there, which on that hour is her, and she is a line cook who has never run anything, and she refused an offer of money a week earlier and said then that the heat was what the refusing costs, and what she has agreed to is a larger version of the same and she has a cloth and a kettle and not nine words, and she asked for it to be written down by somebody other than her, and nobody wrote it down.** At about twenty past seven a telephone rang in that room and a letting clerk of thirty-nine in another building gave her sentence about a vacant frontage and said the word *hold on* for the sixth time in about twelve weeks and put the call through; the caller was wet, had a dog, and said the plate was the only number on any door for about four hundred metres; he was given the address of a hall with a hole in the roof and told to say the front of it was dry, and the dog went to the front. Nobody thanked her. She is not thanked on any of the five and has said three times that she will not be.

### Chapter 0244 — "The Tenth Sitting"

**The Wednesday morning of week eighty-four, in a back room with the shutters up in another district, and on a service road at about ten that night. Load-book entry 247, and its own opening label says this entry is one sitting, one reason, and a number that went up by four.** Ten sittings, nine gaps of four weeks, and the tally of sittings at which nothing has been added is nine. **The holder stated, for the first time in nineteen years, that the room is above the line.** Nine chairs in their curve with the two ends turned in, and the ninth against the wall facing away from the room, where it has stood since the sitting before last, put there by a person who has not been in that room since she was fifteen and who is not asked about it and gave no reason and has not been named in that room. **The rule was given whole and unimproved — that a book of what a caller said cannot be written by the people who did not call — given ten times at ten sittings, asked for nine times in nineteen years, and given unasked on two occasions, which are the ninth and the tenth.** A thank-you was offered and refused on the ground that the rule should be thanked to whoever asked for it and nobody has for about nine years. **The telephone count moved from thirty-seven to forty-one, four in four weeks, the highest four-week movement since the count began, announced unasked at about twenty past ten; she has announced it fifteen times, ten of which correspond to the ten Wednesdays this book holds and five of which predate it and stand on a separate sheet she has never shown anybody, a fact never before stated in that room; the subtraction was done on the fingers twice and produced the same figure and will not be done again in front of nine people; the figure is about her and has never been about the room, and the room is older than the book.** The tenth reason that room has given for not opening a book, and the first about a door rather than a person: on the Friday a man telephoned that desk and asked whether a room behind a door carrying a plate with a telephone number and no name on it was in the book, having been put through by a clerk who said hold on; he gave a street and not a room's name; she cannot answer without opening the book on a table in front of nine people and will not do that to find out whether a man in a wet street is on a page; she has had it since about eleven o'clock on the Friday, has not slept properly, has told nobody, will not ring him, and will not thank anybody in that room who does. **A man of about thirty-one in the back row asked in advance whether she would be the person a flood telephones instead of a room; she said she will be the one who knows that it is a room and not a person, and said she would say it in more than nine words so that nobody counted; two people counted, one at fifteen and one at fourteen; she confirmed fifteen, said it was the longest sentence she has spoken in that room in about four years, said the two people who were there four years ago know that and the seven who were not do not, and declined to explain the difference, and called it a book.** A young man of twenty-one, in attendance since he was seventeen, said the ninth chair had been moved again by somebody who has not been there since she was fifteen, and said he was not reporting it and was saying it because there was a person in the room; she said a chair is a chair; he said he was keeping a count and was not to be confirmed; the chair was against the wall at the end of the morning. Her last sentence, written afterwards in a private notebook that is not the book: at forty-one calls and forty-five lines the book stands ahead of the telephone for the first time since both were kept, it is not a coincidence, it does not fill while it is shut, and the day it is opened is the day the room begins to fill, and neither is a thing she can hold and put down. She did not repeat, on that occasion, what she said in the week of seventy-two about a book that grows.

### Chapter 0245 — "Three Pieces And A Refusal On Purpose"

**The Thursday evening of week eighty-four, ten days into the unheated fortnight, with water standing over the pavement at the low end of three districts. Load-book entry 248, and its own opening label says this entry is four pieces, one owner of none of them, and one refusal made on purpose.** A man of fifty-one with a tool bag came in at half past six wet to the thigh, said the low end of the row was under about four inches of pavement and rising about an inch an hour, and that the basement pump had been reported for the fourth time that week and was still not the fault, and then went out again and nobody stopped him and nobody thanked him. **A man of twenty-nine who works on lifts, in before anybody else, with a page of his own in the ring binder, on an employer's Friday list that is not his and that he does not own, laid out his piece in four sentences: about four hundred buildings on that row that a person cannot get out of on their own, and about nine hundred that they can, and he can get into the four hundred and cannot get anybody out of them; and in the same four sentences he declined to sit on a landing for eleven hours being fine at it where a person might see him being fine at it. A cook of twenty-seven said her piece in about eleven seconds — a kettle, a gas ring that came with the unit, about nine hundred cups, a table and a chair, about four hundred cups in about two days, and hands if the cups run out — and said that is not a piece of a flood, that is tea, and that it is the reason about four hundred people will be standing somewhere dry with something hot in them, and if somebody wants to write that down she would rather they wrote down what a chair is. A first-year of nineteen said it back in nine words: that is the piece, that is all of her piece.** The first-year took the door and the telephone on the plate and said her piece in the plainest sentence anybody had used in that room in a term, and distinguished three things: that answering a telephone is not being a key-holder, that a key is not a telephone, and that she can be asked to stop. **A woman of twenty-four, a nurse, with a clipboard nobody asked her to bring and nothing on it, said her piece in about thirty words: about nine stairs from a bed into a dry hall, and not a building, not a street, not a night; a list of people who cannot be got out of their own homes; about nine spoken to this year; one man in a first-floor flat in that row who will not open his door to a stranger, unsolved and not to be solved that night with a kettle; and that what a flood will actually want is a person and not a room, and that this is a rota and not a moral question, and that nobody in this city has a rota for a thing nobody has agreed is a thing.** The agreeing took about eleven minutes and produced four pieces and no owner. **The man of twenty-two declined a piece and gave the reason twice in public, and then said, unprompted, that he has been waiting about two days for somebody to ask him to run one and nobody did, and the answer would have been the same, and he would like the waiting on this record before the day.** At about eight a first-year of nineteen went out to the service road and telephoned the man of thirty-four who works nights, having been told at half past seven that a cook had offered him a standing Tuesday; she said on return that she had taken the offer and was not sorry. He arrived at nine wet through. **He said he wanted about four seconds, and then said, unprompted and at length, that a telephone call was not what he wanted, that he had known that since the spring, that what he wanted was a witness and that company and a witness are different things, and that he has had neither and has instead got a first-year of nineteen on a service road in the rain, which he called worse. A cook of twenty-seven said one word that was not agreement, and the word was late.** **The first refusal, at about half past eight, on purpose, in public, before about nine people: the man of twenty-nine put his employer's folded Friday list face up on the counter and refused to read a single name off it, take it off his employer's book, photograph it, or enter any person from it on any list that room makes. He gave the grounds — about eleven people a week appear on that page and do not appear on it for anything they have done, a system in about nine places chooses job numbers, his own name is on it, the man with the docket is on it, and the woman who answers a telephone in another building is not, and he has checked — and he said that he was saying no on purpose, at night, in front of witnesses, when saying yes would have been easier and would have worked, and that in about four years somebody will find out that he was asked and did not say.** He put the page back in his coat. No list was read and no name left that room.

### Chapter 0246 — "The Evacuation Still Works"

**The Friday of week eighty-four, the second Friday rota of this movement, with water standing at the end of a service road in three districts. Load-book entry 249, and its own opening label says this entry is one day, one public refusal, and four hundred and eleven cups.** Eleven on the docket and ten in date; the eleventh is a stair fire door whose closer he adjusted for the fifth time in about a fortnight, which he attended at about seven in the morning in about nine inches of water, could not reach, and recorded with the reason; a district man of thirty-eight read that entry on a screen in a different building and said, to nobody, that it was the first in about five months to give a reason for something not done. **The rate did not reach eleven for the first time in about a term and a half.** A man of fifty-one with a tool bag was in the room at seven, at nine and at about two, was not there for most of the time between, and said on the first occasion that a door closer is a service item inside a building and a person is not. **A man of fifty-eight with a bad hip came in at about nine having come down four floors in the dark with a plastic bag containing a pair of shoes resoled eleven times on the left and not on the right, cut by hand, and asked that they be given to somebody in the room with wet feet and no shoes. A cook of twenty-seven did not touch the bag and said the sentence she said about a pair of shoes about five weeks ago: the room can only return it, not transfer it. He took the bag, declined to leave it, was offered no help and asked for none, and came back at about six to say a man whose size it was and who had no shoes now has them, that he is not going to say where, and that a first-year of nineteen said so first.** **The second refusal, at about eleven in the morning, in public, unasked and before anybody had worked out that she was the obvious candidate: a woman of twenty-four would not be the person the telephone on that plate reaches, on the ground that she has been the person in a room that things happen to for about six years, that she holds one of the few lists in this city, that about nine people on it cannot be got out of their own homes, and that one of them is a man in a first-floor flat who will not open his door to a stranger, and that if she is on that line then in about four years somebody in an office will use her as a reason and he will not open the door either. She then named the cost of her own refusal unasked: about four minutes during which nobody could have telephoned her about a man and a first-floor flat, which she will carry, will not hand to anybody, and will not check, and is not the sort of person who is thanked for a thing that cost her nothing. A cook of twenty-seven told her she had just cost that room its evening.
